<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> At the critical inverse temperature <i>β</i> = 1, we prove slow mixing for zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick heat-bath dynamics from typical equilibrium configurations held fixed as initial states. For every deterministic sequence $`t_n=o(n^{2/3})`$ in rate-one-per-site time, the Gibbs mass of initial states whose time-<i>t</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> total-variation distance from equilibrium exceeds 1/4 tends to one in probability over the disorder. The same statement holds for every deterministic integer sequence $`k_n=o(n^{5/3})`$ of uniform-site update attempts.

- **claim-realized-equilibrium-starts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-gibbs-start)): For every deterministic sequence $t_n\ge0$ with $t_n=o(n^{2/3})$, $$\mu_W\{x:d_{t_n}^{\rm ct}(W,x)>1/4\} \xrightarrow{\mathbb P}1.$$ For every deterministic integer sequence $k_n\ge0$ with $k_n=o(n^{5/3})$, $$\mu_W\{x:d_{k_n}^{\rm dt}(W,x)>1/4\} \xrightarrow{\mathbb P}1.$$ The convergence is in probability over the disorder.
  - Explanation: At each prescribed deterministic time below the stated critical scale, the Gibbs mass of fixed starting configurations remaining separated from equilibrium tends to one in disorder probability. Continuous time and attempted updates use different scales.
- **claim-covariance-linear-scale** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-scale)): For every positive deterministic sequence $M_n\to\infty$, with probability tending to one both $\\|\Sigma\\|$ and $\mathcal R_{\rm lin}(\mu_W)$ belong to $$\left[\frac{n^{2/3}}{M_n},\ M_n n^{2/3}\right].$$
  - Explanation: The covariance norm and the maximal linear inverse Rayleigh quotient share the critical scale up to every prescribed deterministic divergent factor, with probability tending to one.
- **claim-critical-mixing-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-mixing)): For every fixed $\epsilon>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P\!\left\{n^{2/3-\epsilon}\le t_{\rm mix} \le e^{\epsilon n}\right\}&\longrightarrow1, \label{eq:continuous-main}\\ \mathbb P\!\left\{n^{5/3-\epsilon}\le k_{\rm mix} \le e^{\epsilon n}\right\}&\longrightarrow1. \label{eq:discrete-main} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Worst-start mixing has the stated polynomial lower bounds and subexponential upper bounds in probability over disorder. The upper and lower bounds do not identify matching mixing exponents within this paper.
- **claim-heatbath-forms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-heatbath-form)): Each $P_i$ is an orthogonal projection in $L^2(\mu)$. The unscaled Dirichlet form is $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal D_\mu(f) &=-\langle f,\mathcal Lf\rangle_\mu =\sum_i\mu[(f-P_if)^2] \label{eq:heatbath-form}\\ &=\sum_i\inf_{h_i:\{-1,1\}^{n-1}\to\mathbb R} \mu[(f-h_i(x_{-i}))^2].\nonumber \end{aligned}$$ The discrete form is $\langle f,(\mathrm{Id}-P)f\rangle_\mu=\mathcal D_\mu(f)/n$, and $P$ has spectrum in $[0,1]$. For a linear function, $$\label{eq:linear-form} \mathcal D_\mu(a^Tx)=\sum_i a_i^2\alpha_i, \qquad \alpha_i=\mu[\mathop{\mathrm{sech}}^2 g_i],\quad g_i(x)=\sum_{j\ne i}W_{ij}x_j.$$
  - Explanation: Conditional expectation gives projection operators and a conditional-variance expression for the heat-bath form. Linear observables have a diagonal coefficient-weighted form, and the discrete form includes the clock normalization.
- **claim-eigenvalue-good-event** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-spectral)): Let $W$ have the GOE normalization of Section <a href="#sec:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:model">2</a>, and write its eigenvalues as $\lambda_1\ge\cdots\ge\lambda_n$. There are events $\mathcal G_n(L_n)$, measurable with respect to these eigenvalues alone, such that $$\mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal G_n(L_n)^c\bigr) \le C\exp(-cL_n^{1/4})$$ for all sufficiently large $n$. On the same events, for every fixed $M<\infty$, $$\begin{aligned} &\lambda_j=2+o(s_0)\quad(1\le j\le3), &&\max_i\|\lambda_i\|\le3,\\ &\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\frac1{2+s-\lambda_i} =R(s)+o(\sqrt s), &&\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\frac1{(2+s-\lambda_i)^2} \le\frac{C_M}{\sqrt s} \quad(s_0\le s\le M). \end{aligned} \label{eq:spectral-estimates}$$ All denominators in these formulas are positive. The little-oh terms have deterministic bounds, uniformly over the event: the first three eigenvalue errors divided by $s_0$ are at most $C(L_n^{-1}+\eta_n)$, and the first resolvent error divided by $\sqrt s$ is at most $C_M(L_n^{-3/2}+\eta_n)$. The event does not depend on $M$.
  - Explanation: An event depending only on ordered eigenvalues controls the leading eigenvalues and resolvent traces uniformly above the enlarged edge scale. The event is independent of the fixed upper resolvent parameter.
- **claim-independent-tridiagonal-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-tridiagonal)): The eigenvalues of $W$ have the same law as those of a symmetric tridiagonal matrix $T$ whose entries $$T_{ii}\sim N(0,2/n),\qquad T_{i,i+1}=T_{i+1,i}\sim\frac{\chi_{n-i}}{\sqrt n} \quad(1\le i<n)$$ are independent. Here $\chi_m$ is the Euclidean norm of $m$ independent standard normal variables.
  - Explanation: The GOE spectrum can be represented using a tridiagonal matrix with independent Gaussian diagonal entries and scaled chi off-diagonal entries. This is equality in spectral law.
- **claim-uniform-noise-energy-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-form-bound)): There is a universal constant $C_0$ such that, with $$\Phi_n(x)=C_0n^{-1/2}A_n(x+s_0)^{1/4} \sqrt{1+\log(1+x/s_0)},\qquad x\ge0,$$ the matrix $H=T-D$ satisfies, simultaneously for every unit vector $u\in\mathbb R^n$, $$\|u^THu\|\le\Phi_n(u^TEu) \label{eq:form-bound}$$ on an event $\mathcal F_n$ with $\mathbb P(\mathcal F_n^c)\le C\exp(-cL_n^{1/4})$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Moreover, $$\sup_{x\ge0}\|\Phi_n'(x)\|\le C\eta_n, \qquad \Phi_n(0)\le C\eta_ns_0. \label{eq:phi-smallness}$$
  - Explanation: A common noise event bounds every unit-vector quadratic form by its deterministic confinement energy and controls the derivative of the scalar error function.
- **claim-hermite-resolvent-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-deterministic-resolvent)): Let $e_1\le\cdots\le e_n$ be the eigenvalues of $E=2I-D$, and set $$R_D(s)=\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(s+E)^{-1},\qquad T_D(s)=\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(s+E)^{-2}=-R_D'(s).$$ For every fixed $M<\infty$ and all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly for $s_0\le s\le M$, $$T_D(s)\le C_Ms^{-1/2},\qquad 0\le R(s)-R_D(s)\le\frac{C_M}{ns}. \label{eq:deterministic-traces}$$ Moreover, $$N(t):=\#\{i:e_i\le t\}\le Cnt^{3/2} \qquad(t\ge s_0). \label{eq:deterministic-counting}$$
  - Explanation: The deterministic reference spectrum obeys resolvent trace and eigenvalue counting bounds throughout the specified enlarged edge regime.
- **claim-exact-spherical-gaussian-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spherical-identity)): For every $a\in[0,2]$, the infimum defining $B(a)$ has a unique minimizer $z_a$. With $$\label{eq:spherical-saddle} k_i(a)=(z_a-a\lambda_i)^{-1}, \qquad \sum_{i=1}^n k_i(a)=n,$$ one has $$1+a\lambda_n\le z_a\le1+a\lambda_1, \qquad \frac1{13}\le k_i(a)\le n.$$ Let $Y_i$ be independent $N(0,k_i(a))$ variables, and let $p_a$ be the density of $\sum_iY_i^2$. Then $$\label{eq:spherical-identity} S(a)=e^{nB(a)} \frac{p_a(n)}{p_{\chi_n^2}(n)}.$$ The conditional law of $Y$ given $\\|Y\\|^2=n$, defined by radial disintegration, is $\sigma_{\Lambda,a}$.
  - Explanation: The spherical variational problem has a unique saddle. Independent Gaussians with the resulting variances, conditioned by radial disintegration, give the tilted spherical law and an exact partition identity.
- **claim-weighted-square-density-at-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mean-density)): There is a universal constant $c>0$ such that, for every $n\ge3$ and every $k_1,\ldots,k_n>0$ with $\sum_i k_i=n$, the density $p$ of $\sum_i k_iG_i^2$, for independent standard Gaussians $G_i$, satisfies $$\label{eq:mean-density} p(n)\ge c\left(\sum_i k_i^2\right)^{-1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: The density at the mean of a positive weighted Gaussian-square sum has a universal lower bound governed by the Euclidean norm of its weights. The proof includes arrays with a dominant weight.
- **claim-sphere-hyperplane-partition-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-partition-bounds)): There are universal constants $c,C>0$ such that, for every $n\ge3$, every ordered spectrum $\lambda_1\ge\cdots\ge\lambda_n$ satisfying $\max_i\|\lambda_i\|\le3$, and every $a\in[0,2]$, $$c n^{-1/2}e^{nB(a)} \le S(a)\le C n^{1/2}e^{nB(a)}.$$ If $H\subset\mathbb R^n$ is any subspace of dimension $n-1$, and $\sigma_{n,H}$ is uniform probability on $\{u\in H:\\|u\\|^2=n\}$, then $$S_H(a):=\int e^{a u^T\Lambda u/2}\,\sigma_{n,H}(du) \le C n^{1/2}e^{nB(a)}.$$ The same constant $C$ works for all $H$ and all $a\in[0,2]$.
  - Explanation: Bounded spectra give polynomial-prefactor partition comparisons on the full sphere and a uniform upper comparison on every hyperplane through the origin.
- **claim-critical-gaussian-parameters** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-critical-sphere)): With the preceding notation, $$\label{eq:critical-spherical-parameters} \lambda_1<z_1<2+s_0,\qquad \left(\sum_i k_i^2\right)^{1/2}\le Cn\sqrt{s_0}, \qquad k_j\ge c/s_0\quad(1\le j\le3).$$ Moreover, $$p(n)\ge \frac{c}{n\sqrt{s_0}}, \qquad \sup_{r\in\mathbb R}p_{-i}(r)\le Cs_0 \quad(1\le i\le n).$$
  - Explanation: On the preceding good spectra, the critical saddle lies below the explicit edge comparison point. The leading variances and radial densities satisfy the bounds needed for coordinate deletion and conditioning.
- **claim-spherical-small-projections** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-spherical-small-ball)): For every $b>0$, the preceding good spectra satisfy $$\label{eq:spherical-small-ball} \sigma_\Lambda\{\|u_1\|\le b n^{1/3}\} \le C L_n^2 b.$$ In particular, given any positive deterministic $b_n\to0$, one can choose $L_n\to\infty$ with $L_n\le\log n$ and $L_n^2b_n\to0$, so that the probability in <a href="#eq:spherical-small-ball" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spherical-small-ball">[eq:spherical-small-ball]</a> tends to zero uniformly over the resulting good spectra.
  - Explanation: The top spherical coordinate has small mass in a shrinking critical window. Choosing the slowly divergent spectral enlargement after the deterministic window makes the bound vanish uniformly on the resulting good spectra.
- **claim-spherical-coordinate-overlap-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-spherical-covariance)): The probability $\sigma_\Lambda$ has mean zero and diagonal covariance in the eigenbasis of $\Lambda$. For every coordinate, $$\label{eq:spherical-coordinate-moment} \int u_i^2\,\sigma_\Lambda(du) \le \frac{Cs_0 k_i}{p(n)}.$$ Consequently, for independent $U,V$ with law $\sigma_\Lambda$, $$\label{eq:spherical-overlap} \mathbb E\left(\frac{U^TV}{n}\right)^2 \le \frac{Cs_0^2}{n^2p(n)^2}\sum_i k_i^2 \le Cn^{-2/3}L_n^4.$$
  - Explanation: Coordinate sign symmetry diagonalizes spherical covariance. Coordinate conditioning then bounds each variance and the squared overlap of independent tilted spherical samples.
- **claim-cubic-overlap-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cubic-deficit)): There is a universal constant $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly for spectra in $\mathcal G_n$ and $q_0\le q\le1$, $$B(1+q)+B(1-q)-2B(1) \le \frac{q^2}{2}-cq^3. \label{eq:cubic-deficit}$$ One may take $c=1/24$.
  - Explanation: Uniformly on good spectra and above the overlap cutoff, the variational pair exponent has a cubic deficit below the quadratic overlap cost. This suppresses the large-overlap tail.
- **claim-conditional-partition-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-orientation-concentration)): For spectra in $\mathcal G_n$, $$\mathbb E_O I=S(1), \qquad \sup_{\Lambda\in\mathcal G_n} \mathbb E_O\!\left[\left(\frac{I}{S(1)}-1\right)^2\right]\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular, under the GOE law, $I/S(1)\to1$ in probability.
  - Explanation: Conditional Haar averaging gives the spherical first moment and vanishing relative partition variance uniformly over good spectra. The normalized partition ratio therefore converges in probability under GOE disorder.
- **claim-ising-top-projection-small-ball** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-static-small-ball)): Let $W$ be the augmented GOE matrix and let $v$ be a unit top eigenvector. For every positive deterministic sequence $b_n\to0$, $$\mu\{x:\|v^Tx\|\le b_n n^{1/3}\} \longrightarrow0 \quad\text{in probability}. \label{eq:static-small-ball}$$ The probability here includes the auxiliary diagonal used to define $v$; the Gibbs measure itself does not depend on that diagonal.
  - Explanation: The top GOE eigenvector projection under the Ising Gibbs law avoids every prescribed shrinking critical window in probability. The test direction may depend on the auxiliary diagonal although the Gibbs law does not.
- **claim-quenched-overlap-tightness** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-overlap)): Let $Q_n=\mathbb E_{\mu^{\otimes2}}q(x,y)^2$. Then $n^{2/3}Q_n$ is bounded in probability. Equivalently, for every positive deterministic sequence $T_n\to\infty$, $$\mathbb P\{n^{2/3}Q_n>T_n\}\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:orientation-overlap-tail}$$ This assertion concerns the original off-diagonal SK disorder.
  - Explanation: The critically rescaled quenched squared overlap is tight under the original off-diagonal disorder. Every deterministic divergent threshold is admissible.
- **claim-two-sided-covariance-scale** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-covariance-scale)): For every positive deterministic sequence $M_n\to\infty$, $$\mathbb P\!\left\{ \frac{n^{2/3}}{M_n}\le\\|\Sigma\\|\le M_n n^{2/3} \right\}\longrightarrow1.$$
  - Explanation: The squared-overlap estimate gives the covariance upper bound, while top-projection anti-concentration gives the lower bound. Both hold simultaneously at every deterministic divergent threshold.
- **claim-stationary-increment-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stationary-increments)): Fix the disorder and a function $f:\{-1,1\}^n\to\mathbb R$. Let $(X_t)_{t\ge0}$ be the continuous-time heat-bath chain with generator $\mathcal L$, and let $(Y_k)_{k\ge0}$ be the discrete-time chain with transition operator $P$, each started from $\mu$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_\mu\bigl[(f(X_t)-f(X_0))^2\bigr] &\le 2t\,\mathcal D_\mu(f), \qquad t\ge0,\label{eq:stationary-increments-ct}\\ \mathbb E_\mu\bigl[(f(Y_k)-f(Y_0))^2\bigr] &\le \frac{2k}{n}\,\mathcal D_\mu(f), \qquad k\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}.\label{eq:stationary-increments-dt} \end{aligned}$$ Here the expectations average only over the initial configuration and the trajectory. In particular, if $g(x)=v^Tx$ and $\\|v\\|=1$, the two right-hand sides are at most $2t$ and $2k/n$, respectively.
  - Explanation: For fixed disorder and a stationary heat-bath trajectory, mean-square increments are bounded by elapsed time times the appropriate Dirichlet form. Unit linear projections have a deterministic form bound.
- **claim-linear-weighted-covariance-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-linear-scale)): With probability tending to one, $$\label{eq:linear-covariance-comparison} \\|\Sigma\\|\le\mathcal R_{\mathrm{lin}}(\mu) \le16\\|\Sigma\\|+n^{13/25}.$$ In particular, $n^{-2/3}\mathcal R_{\mathrm{lin}}(\mu)$ is bounded in probability.
  - Explanation: With probability tending to one, the linear inverse Rayleigh quotient is bounded above by the stated covariance comparison and smaller polynomial correction. The lower comparison holds deterministically in the proof.
- **claim-imported-high-temperature-gap** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-high-temperature-gap)): Fix $0<\beta<1$. Let $J$ be a symmetric matrix with zero diagonal and independent entries $J_{ij}\sim N(0,\beta^2/n)$ for $i<j$, and let $$\nu_J(x)=Z_J^{-1}\exp\bigl(\tfrac12x^TJx\bigr), \qquad x\in\{-1,1\}^n.$$ There is a finite constant $C_\beta$, depending only on $\beta$, such that $$\mathbb P_J\!\left\{ \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\nu_J}(f)\le C_\beta\mathcal D_{\nu_J}(f) \text{ for every }f:\{-1,1\}^n\to\mathbb R \right\}\longrightarrow1.$$ Here $\mathcal D_{\nu_J}$ is the unscaled sum of conditional variances. Consequently, the discrete-time heat-bath chain choosing one uniform site has spectral gap at least $1/(nC_\beta)$ with probability tending to one.
  - Explanation: The cited companion supplies a heat-bath Poincaré inequality simultaneously for all functions at each fixed subcritical inverse temperature, with probability tending to one. Its temperature-dependent constant is not a uniform near-critical estimate.
- **claim-density-ratio-form-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-density-comparison)): Let $\rho,\nu$ be probability measures with full support on $\{-1,1\}^n$. Suppose $$0<m\le\frac{\rho(x)}{\nu(x)}\le M<\infty \qquad\text{for every }x.$$ If $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\nu(f)\le C_\nu\mathcal D_\nu(f)$ for every $f$, then $$\label{eq:density-comparison} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\rho(f)\le \frac{M}{m}C_\nu\mathcal D_\rho(f) \qquad\text{for every }f.$$ In particular, for $\rho(x)\propto e^{H(x)}$ and $\nu(x)\propto e^{\beta H(x)}$, with $\beta<1$, one may take $M/m=e^{(1-\beta)\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}H}$.
  - Explanation: Full-support measures with bounded density ratio transfer a conditional-variance Poincaré inequality with the extreme density-ratio loss. Exponentially tilted versions of the same energy give the stated oscillation factor.
- **claim-subexponential-mixing-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-subexponential-upper)): For every fixed $\epsilon>0$, $$\mathbb P\left\{t_{\mathrm{mix}}\le e^{\epsilon n},\quad k_{\mathrm{mix}}\le e^{\epsilon n}\right\} \longrightarrow1.$$
  - Explanation: For every fixed positive exponential tolerance, both heat-bath clocks satisfy the asserted upper mixing bound with probability tending to one. The argument fixes the comparison temperature before taking the dimension limit.
- **claim-arbitrary-refresh-mixing-time** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-arbitrary-refresh)): Fix any realization of the SK measure on $\Omega_n$, $n\ge2$. For every positive integer $r$, there is an irreducible, aperiodic, reversible Markov kernel with stationary law $\mu$ whose worst-state threshold-$1/4$ mixing time equals $r$.
  - Explanation: A mixture of holding and full equilibrium refresh can realize any prescribed positive integer mixing time while preserving irreducibility, aperiodicity and reversibility. Equilibrium alone therefore does not determine mixing.
- **claim-two-spin-no-essential-upper-bound** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-prop-two-spin)): For $n=2$ and the discrete uniform-site heat-bath kernel, the mixing time has no finite deterministic essential upper bound over the Gaussian coupling, although it is finite for every realization.
  - Explanation: At fixed spin dimension, unbounded Gaussian coupling tails preclude a finite deterministic essential upper mixing bound, even though every individual finite-coupling chain mixes.

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-form-bound",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-deterministic-resolvent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $e_1\\le\\cdots\\le e_n$ be the eigenvalues of $E=2I-D$, and set $$R_D(s)=\\frac1n\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(s+E)^{-1},\\qquad\n T_D(s)=\\frac1n\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(s+E)^{-2}=-R_D'(s).$$ For every fixed $M<\\infty$ and all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly for $s_0\\le s\\le M$, $$T_D(s)\\le C_Ms^{-1/2},\\qquad\n 0\\le R(s)-R_D(s)\\le\\frac{C_M}{ns}.\n \\label{eq:deterministic-traces}$$ Moreover, $$N(t):=\\#\\{i:e_i\\le t\\}\\le Cnt^{3/2}\n \\qquad(t\\ge s_0).\n \\label{eq:deterministic-counting}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-227.5"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For every $a\\in[0,2]$, the infimum defining $B(a)$ has a unique minimizer $z_a$.       "conclusion": {

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stationary-increments",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the disorder and a function $f:\\{-1,1\\}^n\\to\\mathbb R$. Let $(X_t)_{t\\ge0}$ be the continuous-time heat-bath chain with generator $\\mathcal L$, and let $(Y_k)_{k\\ge0}$ be the discrete-time chain with transition operator $P$, each started from $\\mu$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_\\mu\\bigl[(f(X_t)-f(X_0))^2\\bigr]\n &\\le 2t\\,\\mathcal D_\\mu(f), \\qquad t\\ge0,\\label{eq:stationary-increments-ct}\\\\\n \\mathbb E_\\mu\\bigl[(f(Y_k)-f(Y_0))^2\\bigr]\n &\\le \\frac{2k}{n}\\,\\mathcal D_\\mu(f),\n \\qquad k\\in\\{0,1,2,\\ldots\\}.\\label{eq:stationary-increments-dt}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here the expectations average only over the initial configuration and the trajectory. In particular, if $g(x)=v^Tx$ and $\\|v\\|=1$, the two right-hand sides are at most $2t$ and $2k/n$, respectively.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stationary-increments",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the disorder and a function $f:\\{-1,1\\}^n\\to\\mathbb R$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_\\mu\\bigl[(f(X_t)-f(X_0))^2\\bigr]\n &\\le 2t\\,\\mathcal D_\\mu(f), \\qquad t\\ge0,\\label{eq:stationary-increments-ct}\\\\\n \\mathbb E_\\mu\\bigl[(f(Y_k)-f(Y_0))^2\\bigr]\n &\\le \\frac{2k}{n}\\,\\mathcal D_\\mu(f),\n \\qquad k\\in\\{0,1,2,\\ldots\\}.\\label{eq:stationary-increments-dt}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here the expectations average only over the initial configuration and the trajectory. In particular, if $g(x)=v^Tx$ and $\\|v\\|=1$, the two right-hand sides are at most $2t$ and $2k/n$, respectively.",
          "source_id": "PM-227.5"

        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\beta<1$. Let $J$ be a symmetric matrix with zero diagonal and independent entries $J_{ij}\\sim N(0,\\beta^2/n)$ for $i<j$, and let $$\\nu_J(x)=Z_J^{-1}\\exp\\bigl(\\tfrac12x^TJx\\bigr),\n \\qquad x\\in\\{-1,1\\}^n.$$ There is a finite constant $C_\\beta$, depending only on $\\beta$, such that $$\\mathbb P_J\\!\\left\\{\n   \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{\\nu_J}(f)\\le C_\\beta\\mathcal D_{\\nu_J}(f)\n   \\text{ for every }f:\\{-1,1\\}^n\\to\\mathbb R\n \\right\\}\\longrightarrow1.$$ Here $\\mathcal D_{\\nu_J}$ is the unscaled sum of conditional variances.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-density-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho,\\nu$ be probability measures with full support on $\\{-1,1\\}^n$. Suppose $$0<m\\le\\frac{\\rho(x)}{\\nu(x)}\\le M<\\infty\n \\qquad\\text{for every }x.$$ If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\nu(f)\\le C_\\nu\\mathcal D_\\nu(f)$ for every $f$, then $$\\label{eq:density-comparison}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\rho(f)\\le \\frac{M}{m}C_\\nu\\mathcal D_\\rho(f)\n \\qquad\\text{for every }f.$$ In particular, for $\\rho(x)\\propto e^{H(x)}$ and $\\nu(x)\\propto e^{\\beta H(x)}$, with $\\beta<1$, one may take $M/m=e^{(1-\\beta)\\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}H}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-density-comparison",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Critical Ising Gibbs law** ([source](#eq:intro-law)): At inverse temperature $\beta=1$ and zero field, $W_{ij}\sim N(0,1/n)$ for $i<j$, and $$\label{eq:intro-law} \mu_W(x)\propto \exp\!\left(\sum_{i<j}W_{ij}x_ix_j\right), \qquad x\in\{-1,1\}^n.$$
- **Heat-bath operators and clocks** ([source](#sec:model)): For functions $f,g:\Omega_n\to\mathbb R$, write $$\langle f,g\rangle_\mu=\mu[fg], \qquad P_i f=\mathbb E_\mu[f\mid x_{-i}], \qquad \mathcal L=\sum_{i=1}^n(P_i-\mathrm{Id}), \quad P=\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^nP_i.$$
- **Unscaled conditional-variance form** ([source](#stmt-lem-heatbath-form)): The unscaled Dirichlet form is $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal D_\mu(f) &=-\langle f,\mathcal Lf\rangle_\mu =\sum_i\mu[(f-P_if)^2] \label{eq:heatbath-form}\\ &=\sum_i\inf_{h_i:\{-1,1\}^{n-1}\to\mathbb R} \mu[(f-h_i(x_{-i}))^2].\nonumber \end{aligned}$$
- **Average conditional spin variance and local field** ([source](#stmt-lem-heatbath-form)): For a linear function, $$\label{eq:linear-form} \mathcal D_\mu(a^Tx)=\sum_i a_i^2\alpha_i, \qquad \alpha_i=\mu[\mathop{\mathrm{sech}}^2 g_i],\quad g_i(x)=\sum_{j\ne i}W_{ij}x_j.$$
- **Pointwise distances and worst-start mixing times** ([source](#sec:model)): Define $$\begin{aligned} d_t^{\rm ct}(W,x)&=\\|e^{t\mathcal L}(x,\cdot)-\mu_W\\|_{\mathrm{TV}},& t_{\rm mix}&=\inf\{t\ge0:\max_xd_t^{\rm ct}(W,x)\le1/4\},\\ d_k^{\rm dt}(W,x)&=\\|P^k(x,\cdot)-\mu_W\\|_{\mathrm{TV}},& k_{\rm mix}&=\min\{k\in\mathbb N\cup\{0\}:\max_xd_k^{\rm dt}(W,x)\le1/4\}. \end{aligned}$$
- **Spherical partition integral and variational value** ([source](#eq:spherical-definitions)): For $0\le a\le2$, define $$\label{eq:spherical-definitions} S(a)=\int e^{a u^T\Lambda u/2}\,\sigma_n(du), \qquad B(a)=\inf_{z>a\lambda_1} \left\{\frac{z-1}{2} -\frac1{2n}\sum_{i=1}^n\log(z-a\lambda_i)\right\}.$$
- **Tilted spherical probability** ([source](#eq:spherical-definitions)): The associated tilted probability is $$\sigma_{\Lambda,a}(du) =S(a)^{-1}e^{a u^T\Lambda u/2}\,\sigma_n(du), \qquad \sigma_\Lambda=\sigma_{\Lambda,1}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-covariance-lower-scale | \\|\Sigma\\| | ≥ | $\frac{n^{2/3}}{M_n}$ | Every positive deterministic divergent threshold. |
| q-covariance-upper-scale | \\|\Sigma\\| | ≤ | $M_n n^{2/3}$ | Every positive deterministic divergent threshold. |
| q-continuous-mixing-lower | t_{\rm mix} | ≥ | $n^{2/3-\epsilon}$ | Fixed positive exponent slack, followed by the dimension limit. |
| q-discrete-mixing-lower | k_{\rm mix} | ≥ | $n^{5/3-\epsilon}$ | Fixed positive exponent slack, followed by the dimension limit. |
| q-continuous-subexponential-upper | t_{\mathrm{mix}} | ≤ | $e^{\epsilon n}$ | Exponential tolerance fixed before the comparison temperature and dimension limit. |
| q-discrete-subexponential-upper | k_{\mathrm{mix}} | ≤ | $e^{\epsilon n}$ | Exponential tolerance fixed before the comparison temperature and dimension limit. |
| q-spherical-small-window-mass | \sigma_\Lambda\{\|u_1\|\le b n^{1/3}\} | ≤ | $C L_n^2 b$ | Admissible deterministic spectral enlargements and sufficiently large dimension. |
| q-cubic-deficit-constant | c | = | $1/24$ | Overlap above the prescribed cutoff. |
| q-linear-covariance-upper | \mathcal R_{\mathrm{lin}}(\mu) | ≤ | $16\\|\Sigma\\|+n^{13/25}$ | Dimension tending to infinity; quotient restricted to linear observables. |
| q-density-comparison-factor | \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\rho(f) | ≤ | $\frac{M}{m}C_\nu\mathcal D_\rho(f)$ | Supplied density bounds and a valid reference Poincaré constant. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-heatbath-projections** [claim](#stmt-lem-heatbath-form) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heatbath-form): Conditional expectation as orthogonal projection. -> Conditional-variance forms, nonnegative discrete spectrum and linear weights.
- **step-tridiagonal-law** [claim](#stmt-lem-tridiagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tridiagonal): Successive column exposures and orthogonal rotations. -> Independent-entry tridiagonal spectral representation.
- **step-uniform-noise-form** [claim](#stmt-lem-form-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-form-bound): Confinement energy, simultaneous block-prefix concentration and summation by parts. -> Uniform quadratic-form error and derivative control.
- **step-hermite-resolvent** [claim](#stmt-lem-deterministic-resolvent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deterministic-resolvent): Hermite recurrence and a scalar Riccati identity. -> Reference trace and counting bounds.
- **step-eigenvalue-event** [claim](#stmt-prop-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deterministic-resolvent-2): Min–max eigenvalue comparison and dyadic resolvent transfer. -> Eigenvalue-only good event with deterministic spectral errors.
- **step-gaussian-radial-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-spherical-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spherical-identity): Strict convexity of the saddle objective and exact radial density comparison. -> Unique saddle, Gaussian variances and exact conditioned law.
- **step-density-at-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-mean-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mean-density): Contradiction by subsequential compactness, Fourier domination and surviving-coordinate convolution. -> Universal positive lower density bound at the mean.
- **step-partition-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-lem-partition-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-partition-bounds): Radial density bounds and compression interlacing. -> Full-sphere partition bounds and uniform hyperplane upper bound.
- **step-critical-variances** [claim](#stmt-lem-critical-sphere) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-critical-sphere): Compare the exact saddle with the explicit edge point and apply density smoothing. -> Critical variance and residual radial-density estimates.
- **step-spherical-small-window** [claim](#stmt-prop-spherical-small-ball) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spherical-small-ball): Condition a coordinate and compare residual and full radial densities. -> Uniform spherical small-window estimate.
- **step-spherical-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-spherical-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spherical-covariance): Coordinate sign symmetry and weighted coordinate conditioning. -> Coordinate moment and squared-overlap upper bounds.
- **step-variational-cubic-margin** [claim](#stmt-lem-cubic-deficit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cubic-deficit): Differentiate the saddle value and combine quantitative near-critical estimates with bounded convergence away from criticality. -> Uniform cubic overlap deficit.
- **step-haar-partition-concentration** [claim](#stmt-prop-orientation-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-orientation-concentration): Exact Haar moments, cubic tail suppression and disjoint outward overlap bins. -> Uniform conditional relative partition variance tending to zero.
- **step-cube-small-window** [claim](#stmt-thm-static-small-ball) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-static-small-ball): Restricted Haar first moment and normalization by partition concentration. -> Ising top-projection anti-concentration.
- **step-cube-overlap-tightness** [claim](#stmt-prop-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-overlap): Weighted Haar second-moment comparison and threshold-dependent spectral enlargement. -> Tight critically rescaled Ising squared overlap.
- **step-covariance-scale** [claim](#stmt-cor-covariance-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-covariance-scale): Covariance trace identity for the upper bound and top-projection testing for the lower bound. -> Two-sided covariance norm scale at arbitrary divergent thresholds.
- **step-stationary-increments** [claim](#stmt-lem-stationary-increments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stationary-increments): Reversible spectral decomposition and scalar semigroup inequalities. -> Mean-square increment bounds for both clocks.
- **step-realized-start-sign-test** [claim](#stmt-thm-gibbs-start) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stationary-increments-2): Persistent projection signs, state-dependent half-space tests and integration of the intrinsic bad-state indicator. -> Realized equilibrium start obstruction for each deterministic time sequence.
- **step-linear-cavity-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-linear-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-linear-scale): Weighted covariance, exact spin deletion, clipped exceptional-site counting and positive-semidefinite block control. -> High-probability upper comparison of linear inverse Rayleigh quotients with covariance.
- **step-common-linear-scale** [claim](#stmt-thm-linear-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-linear-scale-2): Intersect covariance and cavity comparison events and absorb fixed factors into divergent thresholds. -> Common critical interval for covariance and linear relaxation.
- **step-import-high-temperature-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-high-temperature-gap) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Invoke the separately cited fixed-temperature zero-field Poincaré theorem. -> High-probability all-function Poincaré input with a temperature-dependent constant.
- **step-density-form-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-density-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density-comparison): Compare least-squares infima under bounded density ratios. -> Poincaré transfer with density-ratio loss.
- **step-independent-upper-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-subexponential-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-subexponential-upper): Fix a subcritical comparison temperature, bound energy oscillation and minimum Gibbs mass, then convert spectral decay to total variation. -> Subexponential worst-start mixing upper bounds for both clocks.
- **step-main-worst-start-bounds** [claim](#stmt-thm-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-subexponential-upper-2): Extract worst-start lower bounds from realized starts and intersect with the independent upper event. -> Critical mixing inequalities for both clocks.
- **step-arbitrary-refresh-construction** [claim](#stmt-prop-arbitrary-refresh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arbitrary-refresh): Mix identity with equilibrium refresh and tune the holding probability. -> Reversible kernel with exactly the prescribed mixing threshold.
- **step-two-spin-tail-example** [claim](#stmt-prop-two-spin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-two-spin): Compute a linear eigenobservable and use positive-probability Gaussian coupling tails. -> No finite deterministic essential disorder-uniform mixing upper bound.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-realized-start-obstruction**: At each prescribed deterministic time sequence below the stated clock-dependent scale, the Gibbs mass of realized initial states whose total-variation distance exceeds the threshold tends to one in probability over disorder.
- **invariant-linear-observable-scale**: The covariance norm and the largest inverse Rayleigh quotient among linear observables have the same critical scale up to every positive deterministic divergent factor.
- **invariant-clock-normalization**: The discrete Dirichlet form is the unscaled conditional-variance form divided by the number of sites; stationary increment bounds retain that clock factor.
- **invariant-refresh-distance**: For the whole-configuration refresh construction, the worst-state total-variation distance is the persistence probability raised to the number of steps, multiplied by the complement of the smallest stationary mass.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-average-away-realized-start**: Average the transition law over an equilibrium initial configuration and use that average to assess the realized-start obstruction. -> Stationarity makes the averaged law equal to equilibrium, erasing the conditional distance tested by the theorem.
- **anti-disorder-adaptive-times**: Choose the tested time sequence after observing the disorder and claim the same uniform guarantee. -> The deterministic-sequence quantifier does not establish a simultaneous disorder-adaptive assertion.
- **anti-linear-to-full-gap**: Use the upper bound for linear inverse Rayleigh quotients as an upper bound for the full Poincaré constant. -> Enlarging the observable class can increase the supremum beyond the linear bound.
- **anti-equilibrium-determines-mixing**: Assign a mixing time from the equilibrium Gibbs law without specifying transitions and clock. -> Reversible refresh kernels with the same equilibrium law can have arbitrary prescribed positive integer mixing times.
- **anti-refresh-certifies-critical-asymptotics**: Treat successful finite refresh-kernel checks as verification of the critical heat-bath mixing exponents. -> The checked construction refreshes the whole configuration and does not establish the asymptotic Gaussian-disorder statements for single-site heat-bath dynamics.
- **anti-uniform-disorder-timeout**: Interpret the high-probability asymptotic upper bound as a finite deterministic timeout valid for every coupling realization. -> Unbounded Gaussian couplings prevent a finite deterministic essential upper bound even for the two-spin heat-bath chain.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The arbitrary-refresh proposition supplies an explicit finite reversible kernel and an exact distance identity. The executable scope is its rational two-spin stationary-law subfamily, checked by exact transition-matrix powers against the independent closed-form distance. It does not certify the main asymptotic heat-bath theorems or the arbitrary-target refresh parameter for every real Gibbs law.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with weights, p, and k; weights are positive rational strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: Four weights [a,b,b,a], 0 < p <= 1, integer 0 <= k <= 12.
# OUTPUTS: JSON containing the exact rational worst-state total-variation distance tv.
# TIME: Polynomial in k and the finite transition-matrix dimension; exact arithmetic costs depend on bit length.
# SPACE: A transition matrix and a matrix power, with exact rational entries.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic using Fraction; no floating-point evaluation.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce no certificate.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def solve(data):
    raw = data['weights']
    if len(raw) != 4:
        raise ValueError('four weights required')
    weights = [Fraction(v) for v in raw]
    if any(v <= 0 for v in weights) or weights[0] != weights[3] or weights[1] != weights[2]:
        raise ValueError('positive spin-flip-symmetric weights required')
    p = Fraction(data['p'])
    k = data['k']
    if not (0 < p <= 1) or type(k) is not int or not (0 <= k <= 12):
        raise ValueError('invalid refresh probability or horizon')
    total = sum(weights)
    mu = [v / total for v in weights]
    size = len(mu)
    kernel = [[(1-p) * int(i == j) + p * mu[j] for j in range(size)] for i in range(size)]
    power = [[Fraction(int(i == j)) for j in range(size)] for i in range(size)]
    for unused in range(k):
        power = [[sum(power[i][h] * kernel[h][j] for h in range(size)) for j in range(size)] for i in range(size)]
    distances = [sum(abs(power[i][j] - mu[j]) for j in range(size)) / 2 for i in range(size)]
    return {'tv': str(max(distances))}

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(json.dumps(solve(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

Named vector order: zero-horizon, full-refresh, exact-threshold, uniform-before-threshold, uniform-after-threshold, nonuniform-before-threshold, nonuniform-after-threshold.",

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef main():\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n        data = json.load(handle)\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n        result = json.load(handle)\n    values = tuple(Fraction(x) for x in data['weights'])\n    if len(values) != 4 or min(values) <= 0 or values[0] != values[3] or values[1] != values[2]:\n        return 1\n    p = Fraction(data['p'])\n    k = data['k']\n    if not (0 < p <= 1) or type(k) is not int or not (0 <= k <= 12):\n        return 1\n    stationary_minimum = min(values) / sum(values)\n    persistence = Fraction(1) - p\n    truth = persistence ** k * (Fraction(1) - stationary_minimum)\n    expected = Fraction(sys.argv[3])\n    if not isinstance(result, dict) or set(result) != {'tv'} or not isinstance(result['tv'], str):\n        return 1\n    observed = Fraction(result['tv'])\n    return 0 if observed == truth and expected == truth else 1\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    try:\n        code = main()\n    except Exception:\n        code = 1\n    sys.exit(code)\n",
    "output_contract": "A JSON object with exactly the key tv, whose string value represents the exact worst-state total-variation distance at the requested horizon. The oracle accepts only if that rational equals both the independent refresh formula and the supplied expected rational.",

The executable scope is its rational two-spin stationary-law subfamily, checked by exact transition-matrix powers against the independent closed-form distance. It does not certify the main asymptotic heat-bath theorems or the arbitrary-target refresh parameter for every real Gibbs law.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with weights, p, and k; weights are positive rational strings.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Four weights [a,b,b,a], 0 < p <= 1, integer 0 <= k <= 12.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON containing the exact rational worst-state total-variation distance tv.\n# TIME: Polynomial in k and the finite transition-matrix dimension; exact arithmetic costs depend on bit length.\n# SPACE: A transition matrix and a matrix power, with exact rational entries.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic using Fraction; no floating-point evaluation.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce no certificate.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef solve(data):\n    raw = data['weights']\n    if len(raw) != 4:\n        raise ValueError('four weights required')\n    weights = [Fraction(v) for v in raw]\n    if any(v <= 0 for v in weights) or weights[0] != weights[3] or weights[1] != weights[2]:\n        raise ValueError('positive spin-flip-symmetric weights required')\n    p = Fraction(data['p'])\n    k = data['k']\n    if not (0 < p <= 1) or type(k) is not int or not (0 <= k <= 12):\n        raise ValueError('invalid refresh probability or horizon')\n    total = sum(weights)\n    mu = [v / total for v in weights]\n    size = len(mu)\n    kernel = [[(1-p) * int(i == j) + p * mu[j] for j in range(size)] for i in range(size)]\n    power = [[Fraction(int(i == j)) for j in range(size)] for i in range(size)]\n    for unused in range(k):\n        power = [[sum(power[i][h] * kernel[h][j] for h in range(size)) for j in range(size)] for i in range(size)]\n    distances = [sum(abs(power[i][j] - mu[j]) for j in range(size)) / 2 for i in range(size)]\n    return {'tv': str(max(distances))}\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    print(json.dumps(solve(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The asymptotic mixing bounds do not provide a certified stopping rule for a sampler on a particular coupling realization. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The specified simulation dynamics resample individual spins from a particular Gibbs law. No construction connects these dynamics to game state evolution or a general simulation engine. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The spectral estimates establish asymptotic control on random eigenvalue events, but do not supply a computational diagnostic that certifies whether a given instance has entered the asserted regime. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | For a fixed Gibbs law, the refresh construction explicitly produces a reversible kernel with any prescribed positive integer mixing time. It supplies a finite-state example for checking how transition rules determine convergence, assuming access to exact equilibrium refreshes. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted covariance matrix represents a variational quantity analytically. The paper gives no storage representation, query operations, or update procedure for maintaining that matrix. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The tridiagonal representation preserves an eigenvalue distribution, but supplies no hardware mapping or accelerator resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A total-variation obstruction for spin dynamics does not construct a cryptographic primitive or establish security against an adversary. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The covariance and linear inverse Rayleigh estimates concern equilibrium spin observables under specified heat-bath dynamics. No learning objective, training rule, or generalization guarantee is constructed. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The spherical saddle has a unique minimizer, but the paper supplies no optimisation algorithm, termination certificate, or annealing schedule for finding low-energy spin configurations. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Independent site clocks describe the mathematical update schedule. They do not construct a distributed protocol with communication, consistency, or failure handling. |
| other (Statistical mechanics of disordered spin systems) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The exact Gaussian conditioning construction represents the quadratically tilted spherical equilibrium law. Together with the spherical partition identity, it provides an analytical reference for the transfer of projection and overlap estimates to the Ising model. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-critical-gaussian-zero-field-model** (hypothesis): The critical conclusions concern the zero-field SK law with independent Gaussian pair couplings. They do not establish corresponding conclusions for external fields, sparse interactions or other disorder distributions. Blocked use: ['Transfer the critical mixing and covariance conclusions to a different interaction law without a separate argument.']
- **limit-update-rule-and-clock** (hypothesis): The heat-bath conclusions require the specified conditional resampling rule and clock. Continuous elapsed time and uniform-site update attempts have different normalizations. Blocked use: ['Read continuous time as the same number of discrete update attempts.', 'Assign heat-bath mixing bounds to a chain sharing only its equilibrium law.']
- **limit-realized-start-before-averaging** (formal_scope): The realized equilibrium configuration must be fixed before its transition law is compared with equilibrium. Averaging the transition law over equilibrium initial configurations removes the obstruction being tested. Blocked use: ["Use the equilibrium-averaged transition law to assess the theorem's conditional distances."]
- **limit-deterministic-time-quantifiers** (formal_scope): The realized-start theorem treats prescribed deterministic time sequences separately. It supplies no uniform assertion over disorder-adaptive sequences or a common set of slow starts for every sequence. Blocked use: ['Select a time sequence after observing disorder and invoke the deterministic-sequence theorem.', 'Claim simultaneous slow mixing on the same set of initial configurations for all admissible horizons.']
- **limit-typical-disorder-not-instance-timeout** (counterexample_scope): The asymptotic bounds hold in probability over disorder. They are not deterministic essential upper bounds valid across all Gaussian coupling realizations. Blocked use: ['Turn a high-probability asymptotic upper bound into a guaranteed execution timeout for every instance.']
- **limit-linear-tests-not-full-gap** (formal_scope): The upper bound on linear inverse Rayleigh quotients does not upper-bound the full Poincaré constant. The independent all-function comparison has a different conclusion. Blocked use: ['Enlarge the class of linear observables and retain its upper bound for arbitrary functions.', 'Infer matching full relaxation exponents from the covariance scale alone.']
- **limit-unmatched-critical-mixing-bounds** (formal_scope): The present critical mixing theorem provides polynomial lower bounds and subexponential upper bounds. Matching critical mixing exponents require the separate arguments attributed to the companion. Blocked use: ['Report matching worst-start mixing exponents as a consequence of the present upper comparison.']
- **limit-spectral-enlargement-regime** (hypothesis): The spectral construction uses a positive deterministic enlargement that diverges within its stated logarithmic restriction. Its trace estimates start at the enlarged edge scale and have a fixed finite upper resolvent parameter. Blocked use: ['Apply the trace bounds below their stated lower spectral scale.', 'Replace a fixed upper resolvent parameter by an unrestricted dimension-dependent parameter.']
- **limit-good-spectra-before-conditioning-transfer** (hypothesis): The critical spherical variance, projection and overlap bounds are uniform on the preceding good spectra for sufficiently large dimension. Bounded spectrum alone supplies the general partition comparisons, not all critical conclusions. Blocked use: ['Apply critical coordinate variance or overlap bounds to an arbitrary bounded spectrum.']
- **limit-radial-disintegration-not-unconditioned-sampling** (formal_scope): The spherical law is obtained by radial disintegration at a fixed squared radius. The independent Gaussian vector before conditioning has a different law. Blocked use: ['Replace the tilted spherical law by unconditioned Gaussian samples.', 'Treat exact-radius conditioning as ordinary rejection on an event with positive probability.']
- **limit-positive-weights-density-lemma** (hypothesis): The density-at-the-mean lemma assumes positive Gaussian-square weights with their prescribed sum and the stated dimension restriction. Its universal constant is established through contradiction and subsequences rather than an explicit numerical value. Blocked use: ['Apply the lemma to signed weights.', 'Use the proof as an explicit numerical lower-density certificate without an additional effective estimate.']
- **limit-hyperplane-bound-is-one-sided** (formal_scope): The hyperplane partition estimate is an upper comparison. Its Gaussian uses the full-space saddle, which need not minimize the compressed variational problem or give the compressed radius its prescribed mean. Blocked use: ['Claim the full-sphere lower partition bound uniformly on every hyperplane from this argument.', 'Treat the full-space saddle as the compressed saddle.']
- **limit-cubic-deficit-overlap-range** (hypothesis): The cubic deficit is asserted on good spectra above the overlap cutoff for sufficiently large dimension. Its macroscopic-range uniformity uses dominated convergence and a subsequence contradiction without an explicit dimension threshold. Blocked use: ['Extend the cubic estimate below its overlap cutoff.', 'Treat the asymptotic argument as a numerical certificate at an arbitrary finite dimension.']
- **limit-haar-first-moment-needs-normalization-control** (hypothesis): Haar first moments transfer unnormalized partition sums. Transfer to normalized Gibbs probabilities additionally requires control of the random partition sum. Blocked use: ['Infer a quenched Gibbs small-projection bound from its restricted Haar first moment alone.', 'Apply the outward-bin comparison to a reweighting without the required nonnegativity, evenness and outward monotonicity.']
- **limit-auxiliary-diagonal-test-direction** (formal_scope): The top spectral direction may depend on the auxiliary Gaussian diagonal even though the Gibbs law and heat-bath distances do not. The realized-start proof removes that diagonal only after bounding an intrinsic bad-state indicator. Blocked use: ['Require the stated top eigenvector to be a function of the original off-diagonal disorder alone.', 'Remove the auxiliary diagonal before preserving the intrinsic distance comparison.']
- **limit-stationary-increment-initial-law** (hypothesis): The increment bounds average over a stationary initial configuration and trajectory for fixed disorder. They do not directly give the same mean-square bound from every deterministic initial state. Blocked use: ['Apply the stationary increment inequality pointwise to an arbitrary initial configuration without the subsequent averaging and sign-test argument.']
- **limit-linear-cavity-conditioning** (hypothesis): The linear upper comparison requires control of reciprocal conditional-variance weights through spin deletion. The exceptional-site argument uses conditioning on the deleted matrix and a clipped count; it assumes neither independence between sites nor conditional Gaussian identities after conditioning on the global good event. Blocked use: ['Infer the linear upper bound from covariance alone.', 'Condition the local Gaussian calculation on the global cavity event or assume independent exceptional-site indicators.']
- **limit-fixed-temperature-import** (hypothesis): The imported high-temperature inequality holds for each fixed subcritical temperature with a temperature-dependent constant. The upper comparison chooses that temperature after the exponential tolerance and before the dimension limit. Blocked use: ['Replace the fixed comparison temperature by a dimension-dependent approach to criticality without a uniform constant estimate.', 'Claim that the imported inequality is proved within the present paper.']
- **limit-density-transfer-preconditions** (hypothesis): The density comparison requires full support, positive finite pointwise density bounds everywhere and a reference inequality covering every function. Its loss uses the extreme density ratio rather than an average discrepancy. Blocked use: ['Transfer the inequality from empirical or average density bounds alone.', 'Use a reference inequality restricted to linear functions as the all-function premise.']
- **limit-refresh-construction-scope** (formal_scope): The arbitrary-refresh construction assumes freedom to choose a whole-configuration refresh kernel and access to the equilibrium law and its minimum mass. It demonstrates dependence on the update mechanism rather than an efficient implementation of heat-bath sampling. Blocked use: ['Use the refresh construction as a certified efficient method for sampling an unknown Gibbs law.', 'Treat finite refresh-kernel checks as verification of the asymptotic single-site heat-bath theorems.']

Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_\\mu\\bigl[(f(X_t)-f(X_0))^2\\bigr]\n &\\le 2t\\,\\mathcal D_\\mu(f), \\qquad t\\ge0,\\label{eq:stationary-increments-ct}\\\\\n \\mathbb E_\\mu\\bigl[(f(Y_k)-f(Y_0))^2\\bigr]\n &\\le \\frac{2k}{n}\\,\\mathcal D_\\mu(f),\n \\qquad k\\in\\{0,1,2,\\ldots\\}.\\label{eq:stationary-increments-dt}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here the expectations average only over the initial configuration and the trajectory. In particular, if $g(x)=v^Tx$ and $\\|v\\|=1$, the two right-hand sides are at most $2t$ and $2k/n$, respectively.",

Consequently, $$\\label{eq:cavity-exceptional-count}\n \\mathbb P(|B|>n^{1/2})\n \\le\\mathbb P(E_n^c)+\\mathbb P(N_n>n^{1/2})\n \\le\\mathbb P(E_n^c)+Cn^{-1/12}\\longrightarrow0.$$ No independence between sites, or conditioning on $E_n$ in the Gaussian calculation, is used.",

          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prop-arbitrary-refresh</summary>\n\n*Proof.* At each step, refresh the whole configuration from $\\mu$ with probability $p$ and otherwise leave it fixed. In operator notation, $\\Pi(x,y)=\\mu(y)$ and $K_p=(1-p)\\mathrm{Id}+p\\Pi$, where $0<p\\le1$. All entries of $K_p$ are positive, and $\\mu(x)K_p(x,y)=\\mu(y)K_p(y,x)$. The identity $\\Pi^2=\\Pi$ gives, for $0<p<1$, $$K_p^k=\\Pi+(1-p)^k(\\mathrm{Id}-\\Pi),\\qquad\n \\max_x\\|K_p^k(x,\\cdot)-\\mu\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n =(1-p)^k(1-\\mu_{\\min}).$$ Here $\\mu_{\\min}\\le2^{-n}\\le1/4$. ### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 227 (`lean/docs/227.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A spectral gap throughout the high-temperature Sherrington–Kirkpatrick phase](../../preprints/A-spectral-gap-throughout-the-high-temperature-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-phase-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Cutoff throughout the high-temperature Sherrington–Kirkpatrick phase](../../preprints/Cutoff-throughout-the-high-temperature-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-phase-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Critical slowing down in the Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model](../../preprints/Critical-slowing-down-in-the-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-model-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Stretched-exponential barriers for typical SK initial states](../../preprints/Stretched-exponential-barriers-for-typical-SK-initial-states-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> For every fixed inverse temperature $0<\beta<1$, the formalization proves a dimension-independent Poincaré inequality for the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model with probability tending to one over the disorder. Equivalently, the unscaled single-site heat-bath spectral gap stays bounded away from zero. For dynamics that choose one site uniformly at each discrete step, the spectral gap is at least $1/(Cn)$ with probability tending to one, for a constant $C$ depending on $\beta$.
>
> The formalized supporting result proves ratio cutoff for discrete single-site heat-bath dynamics in the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model for $0\le\beta<1/2$. For every $0<\varepsilon<1/2$ and $\eta>0$, the probability over the disorder that $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(\varepsilon)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}(1-\varepsilon)>1+\eta$ tends to zero as $n\to\infty$. The denominator is positive for all sufficiently large sizes.
>
> The paper's full range $\beta<1$, explicit cutoff location, and continuous-time conclusion are outside this selected statement.
>
> At criticality in the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model, the formalization proves that for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, the continuous-time mixing time lies between $n^{2/3-\varepsilon}$ and $e^{\varepsilon n}$, and the discrete-time mixing count lies between $n^{5/3-\varepsilon}$ and $e^{\varepsilon n}$, with probability tending to one over the Gaussian disorder. The disorder has independent off-diagonal entries of variance $1/n$. Continuous updates have rate one per site; discrete time counts uniformly chosen single-site update attempts. Mixing is worst-start total variation at threshold $1/4$, with holding allowed.
>
> For deterministic times $t_n=o(n^{2/3})$ or update counts $k_n=o(n^{5/3})$, the Gibbs mass of realized starting states still farther than $1/4$ from equilibrium tends to one in probability. For every deterministic positive sequence $M_n\to\infty$, the covariance operator norm and the linear Rayleigh supremum, using the unscaled heat-bath Dirichlet form, both lie between $n^{2/3}/M_n$ and $M_n n^{2/3}$ with probability tending to one. The covariance result does not give an upper bound for the full inverse spectral gap.
>
> The formalization proves a stretched-exponential mixing obstruction for the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at every fixed inverse temperature $\beta>1$. At time $\exp(n^{1/10000})$, the expected Gibbs mass of initial configurations whose total-variation distance from equilibrium exceeds $1/4$ tends to one. Since this mass lies in $[0,1]$, it also tends to one in probability over the disorder.
>
> The result covers both rate-one-per-site continuous-time heat-bath dynamics and the same stated number of discrete update attempts.

Comparator source `CriticalSKMixing.lean` (2762 bytes, sha256 `16c41c4533f545eb81b40e5698199ea80ecee4e86d5db0b1a5594b51ba1a5499`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open scoped BigOperators Topology NNReal ENNReal
open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter

noncomputable section
namespace CriticalSK

abbrev Spin (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Bool

abbrev Edge (n : ℕ) := {p : Fin n × Fin n // p.1 < p.2}

abbrev Disorder (n : ℕ) := Edge n → ℝ

def spinValue (b : Bool) : ℝ := if b then 1 else -1

def hamiltonian {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ e : Edge n, W e * spinValue (x e.val.1) * spinValue (x e.val.2)

def partition {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ x : Spin n, Real.exp (hamiltonian W x)

def gibbs {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  Real.exp (hamiltonian W x) / partition W

def disorderLaw (n : ℕ) : Measure (Disorder n) :=
  Measure.pi fun _ : Edge n => gaussianReal 0 (n : ℝ≥0)⁻¹

def sameExcept {n : ℕ} (i : Fin n) (x y : Spin n) : Prop :=
  ∀ j, j ≠ i → y j = x j

instance {n : ℕ} (i : Fin n) (x y : Spin n) : Decidable (sameExcept i x y) :=
  Classical.propDecidable _

def siteKernel {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) (i : Fin n) : Matrix (Spin n) (Spin n) ℝ :=
  fun x y => if sameExcept i x y then
    gibbs W y / (∑ z : Spin n, if sameExcept i x z then gibbs W z else 0)
  else 0

def discreteKernel {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) : Matrix (Spin n) (Spin n) ℝ :=
  (n : ℝ)⁻¹ • ∑ i : Fin n, siteKernel W i

def generator {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) : Matrix (Spin n) (Spin n) ℝ :=
  ∑ i : Fin n, (siteKernel W i - 1)

def continuousKernel {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) (t : ℝ) :
    Matrix (Spin n) (Spin n) ℝ :=
  NormedSpace.exp (t • generator W)

def totalVariation {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) (p : Spin n → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∑ y : Spin n, |p y - gibbs W y|

def continuousDistance {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) (t : ℝ) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  totalVariation W (continuousKernel W t x)

def discreteDistance {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) (k : ℕ) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  totalVariation W ((discreteKernel W ^ k) x)

def continuousMixingTime {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) : ℝ :=
  sInf {t : ℝ | 0 ≤ t ∧ ∀ x, continuousDistance W t x ≤ 1 / 4}

def discreteMixingTime {n : ℕ} (W : Disorder n) : ℕ :=
  sInf {k : ℕ | ∀ x, discreteDistance W k x ≤ 1 / 4}

theorem critical_mixing_bounds (ε : ℝ) (hε : 0 < ε) :
    Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => (disorderLaw n {W |
      (n : ℝ) ^ ((2 : ℝ) / 3 - ε) ≤ continuousMixingTime W ∧
      continuousMixingTime W ≤ Real.exp (ε * n)}).toReal) atTop (𝓝 1) ∧
    Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => (disorderLaw n {W |
      (n : ℝ) ^ ((5 : ℝ) / 3 - ε) ≤ (discreteMixingTime W : ℝ) ∧
      (discreteMixingTime W : ℝ) ≤ Real.exp (ε * n)}).toReal) atTop (𝓝 1) := by
  sorry

end CriticalSK
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


At the critical inverse temperature <i>β</i> = 1, we prove slow mixing for zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick heat-bath dynamics from typical equilibrium configurations held fixed as initial states. For every deterministic sequence $`t_n=o(n^{2/3})`$ in rate-one-per-site time, the Gibbs mass of initial states whose time-<i>t</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> total-variation distance from equilibrium exceeds 1/4 tends to one in probability over the disorder. The same statement holds for every deterministic integer sequence $`k_n=o(n^{5/3})`$ of uniform-site update attempts.
